# Audio accuracy, 2026-10-29

Tempo and key accuracy of the audio service (`services/audio`) on annotated datasets, the calibration of its withholding rules and its answers on generated controls, measured by `evals/audio` (`uv run python -m evals_audio`). The harness imports the service and calls its own `analyze_signals`, so the numbers are what the service returns, gate and withholding rules included. Rendered from `audio-2026-10-29.json`, which holds the per-clip results: track ids, estimates and scores. It stores no reference labels and no audio file names, but an estimate with its score often gives the reference away (a MIREX score of 1, 0.5, 0.3 or 0.2 fixes the key; an Acc1 hit pins the tempo within 4 %), so those results are derived from the annotations: the licence and credits under each dataset apply to them. Not run for this report: tempo on GiantSteps Tempo; what the tempo rules compare on GiantSteps Tempo; the golden set; the estimators' costs. Any report in `evals/reports` that holds them states the analyzer version it was made with.

Runs in this report: tempo on Ballroom (ISMIR 2004 tempo induction contest) (librosa, essentia, beat-this); key on GiantSteps Key, as GiantSteps+ (Faraldo 2017 revision) (librosa, essentia); what the tempo rules compare on Ballroom (ISMIR 2004 tempo induction contest) (librosa, essentia, beat-this); what the key rules compare on GiantSteps Key, as GiantSteps+ (Faraldo 2017 revision) (librosa, essentia); the generated controls (librosa, essentia, beat-this). beat-this is measured here only: the service does not ship it, and the service's default estimator is not changed by this report.

## How to read it

- **n** is every selected clip with verified audio and a usable reference. **Answered** clips got a value from the service; **withheld** clips got none; **failed** clips raised an error (none is expected). Coverage is answered / n.
- **correct / n** counts a withheld clip as no correct value. It answers *how often does a user get a right value*. **correct / answered** is the share of returned values that are right. It answers *how often is a value the service shows right*, and it depends on which clips the service chose to answer: two estimators that answer different clips are compared in the paired table, not by this column.
- **Acc1**: the tempo is within 4 % of the reference. **Acc2**: within 4 % of the reference times 1, 2, 3, 1/2 or 1/3 (octave and meter errors forgiven).
- **MIREX** key score: 1 same key, 0.5 a perfect fifth above in the same mode, 0.3 relative, 0.2 parallel, 0 otherwise; shown as the summed score over the denominator. **exact**: the same key only.
- **Intervals** are 95 %: Wilson score intervals for Acc1, Acc2 and exact; a BCa bootstrap (10,000 resamples, fixed seed) for MIREX, with no interval when every score is equal. Each interval rests on the denominator printed next to it, which for correct / answered can be far below n.
- **Paired MIREX difference**: most paired key differences are zero, and a bootstrap interval can then cover far less than 95 %. The interval here is a paired t interval whose spread includes two pseudo-differences at -1 and +1, the ends of the range, so it never collapses to zero width. Each row says how often it covered the mean when that row's own differences were resampled. It is wide at small n and is not conservative in general: on dense differences it can cover less than 95 % (`evals/audio/scripts/paired_coverage.py` simulates both). The sign-flip p-value tests "no difference" over every sign pattern of the nonzero differences (exact up to 16 of them).
- **p-values** are exact where it is cheap (McNemar; sign-flip; the AUC's permutation test up to 200,000 orderings) and Monte Carlo at a fixed seed otherwise, as labelled, computed as (hits + 1) / (resamples + 1). Each is for one comparison. This report gives 30 distinct ones (the default-estimator table repeats some of them); about 1.5 of them are expected under 0.05 by chance alone, so none should be read as a finding without a correction for the number of tests.
- **beat-this** (checkpoint `final0`, CPU, no DBN) is not part of the service; the harness runs it behind the service's estimator interface, so its tempo goes through the service's own rules: the onset-periodicity minimum, 40-208 BPM, the tempo refined from the beats on onsets, and the steadiness check. It gives no confidence (so no curve and no confidence minimum) and no key. Its authors trained `final0` on every dataset they used except GTZAN, Ballroom included: an estimator marked *trained on this dataset* is shown on its own training data: those cells are not evidence about new music and its pair tables there are not printed.
- **Lifted rules**: the service withholds a tempo under its rules (the periodicity and confidence minimums, the steadiness check). The estimate it computed is kept apart and scored, so a comparison can be made with a rule lifted. That is calibration, never what a user gets. The steadiness check compares each third of the window with librosa's own tempo estimate on that third.
- **Comparability**: one 30 s window per clip and the excluded labels make these numbers incomparable with published figures for the same datasets (computed on full excerpts, usually on every labelled track).

## Tempo estimator bake-off

Each estimator's figures from the runs below, side by side: correct / n counts a withheld tempo as not correct, so estimators that answer different clips stay comparable; the paired tests on the same clips are in each run. A cell for an estimator trained on that dataset is marked and is not evidence about new music. The golden-set columns are agreement with Deezer's own estimate, not accuracy.

| estimator | ballroom: Acc1 correct / n | ballroom: Acc2 correct / n |
|---|---|---|
| `librosa` | 109/200 = 54.5 % [47.6, 61.3] | 135/200 = 67.5 % [60.7, 73.6] |
| `essentia` | 106/200 = 53.0 % [46.1, 59.8] | 129/200 = 64.5 % [57.7, 70.8] |
| `beat-this` | 148/200 = 74.0 % [67.5, 79.6] (trained on this dataset) | 148/200 = 74.0 % [67.5, 79.6] (trained on this dataset) |

The figures above are as the service serves them. The withholding rules cost the estimators unequally (see each run's Withheld table):

- ballroom, with only the steadiness check lifted (calibration, not served): Acc1 correct / n `librosa` 118/200 = 59.0 % [52.1, 65.6]; `essentia` 116/200 = 58.0 % [51.1, 64.6]; `beat-this` 164/200 = 82.0 % [76.1, 86.7] (trained on this dataset).
- ballroom, with every withholding rule lifted (calibration, not served): Acc1 correct / n `librosa` 140/200 = 70.0 % [63.3, 75.9]; `essentia` 145/200 = 72.5 % [65.9, 78.2]; `beat-this` 188/200 = 94.0 % [89.8, 96.5] (trained on this dataset).

## Default estimator

The service's default tempo and key estimator is `essentia` (what `estimators.select_estimator()` picks when `AUDIO_ESTIMATOR` is not set, with Essentia installed as in the service's image).

Criterion: an estimator replaces the default when, on data it was not trained on, it gives more correct tempos (Acc1: within 4 %) over n than the default as the service serves them (withholding rules included), with an exact McNemar p under 0.05 on the same clips, and its cost to the image, the memory and the analysis time is worth that gain. The tempos it shows that are wrong (a wrong BPM shown as measured) and the cost are weighed, not thresholded.

Evidence in this report, each candidate against `essentia` on the same clips, on data the candidate was not trained on: correct / n of each, and the tempos each showed that are wrong (out of those it showed), with exact McNemar p-values. The golden row is agreement with Deezer's estimate, not accuracy.

| candidate | data (against `essentia`) | as served: Acc1 correct | as served: Acc2 correct | as served: shown but wrong (Acc1) | as served: shown but wrong even forgiving octave and meter (Acc2) | steadiness check lifted: Acc1 correct | every rule lifted: Acc1 correct |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `librosa` | ballroom | 109 vs 106 of 200, p = 0.607 | 135 vs 129 of 200, p = 0.307 | 30 of 139 shown vs 27 of 133, p = 0.648 | 4 of 139 shown vs 4 of 133, p = 1.000 | 118 vs 116 of 200, p = 0.815 | 140 vs 145 of 200, p = 0.405 |

The same comparison against `essentia` on both halves of the calibration runs' clips and on all of them, as served (the tempo rules these estimators use are the ones before this calibration, so both halves serve this comparison equally). The clips come in groups (artists, labels, tracks cut from the same release), so they are not independent and each p-value overstates the evidence.

| candidate | data | clips | Acc1 correct | right only for the candidate / only for `essentia` | test |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `librosa` | ballroom | calibration | 53 vs 49 of 99 | 4 / 0 | McNemar exact p = 0.125 |
| `librosa` | ballroom | evaluation | 56 vs 57 of 101 | 5 / 6 | McNemar exact p = 1.000 |
| `librosa` | ballroom | all | 109 vs 106 of 200 | 9 / 6 | McNemar exact p = 0.607 |

Decision: the service keeps `essentia` under the current withholding rules. As served, no candidate gives significantly more correct tempos (Acc1 over n) on data it was not trained on. `beat-this` also has no key: the service would need it for tempo and another estimator for key.

## Calibration of the withholding rules

The service withholds a tempo or a key when its rules say the evidence is too weak (the steadiness check, the confidence and periodicity minimums, the key evidence rule and the key strength minimum). This section sets those rules from measured results: on the annotated clips of the calibration runs below and on generated controls whose truth is known by construction. Each selected clip is a **calibration** clip or an **evaluation** clip, fixed by a SHA-256 of the dataset and its track id (about half each). Values are chosen on the calibration clips and the controls; the evaluation clips judge each change once more. The choice is made again here from the recorded clips (`evals_audio.calibration.select`), so it can be checked against the values the service has.

- The procedure was written down before the calibration runs. A **loosening** (a value under which a clip or a control gets a value it did not have) is accepted when its extra answers on the annotated clips are right (Acc1 for tempo, exact for key) with a Wilson 95 % lower bound of at least 50 % (L1), at most 10 points less often than the old answers (L2), wrong even forgiving octave and meter (key: unrelated) at most 10 points more often (L3); the loosest value that passes is taken. A **tightening** is accepted when it removes a value a control shows wrongly (T1), lowers correct / n by at most 2 points on each dataset and withholds answers that are right less often than those it keeps (T2); the least strict value that passes is taken. Either way, no control may go from right to wrong (L4). A rule the service keeps per estimator (the confidence and strength minimums) is decided on that estimator's clips; a shared rule on Essentia's (the default) and checked on librosa's: a change librosa's clips reject is not made. beat-this is shown under the service's rules and decides nothing.
- Only changes that withhold more are shipped. A loosening the procedure picks is shown but not shipped: it rests on these clips alone, and two loosenings chosen this way in earlier versions of this calibration gave keys to material with no key.
- Each change is then judged on the evaluation clips: a loosening that fails L1-L3 or a tightening that fails T2 there is reverted to the value before it. The controls are the ones the choice saw, so a change made for a control is not tested on new controls there; a change that alters no evaluation clip stays.
- The procedure is applied as written before the calibration runs. After a first sweep of the calibration clips, revisions were proposed and used in a first version of this calibration: judging the key strength before the key evidence rules, a family that drops the spectral-line rules over a beat for Essentia, choosing the tightening that fixes most controls, and requiring a tightening to break no control. They were made after calibration results had been seen; the line-rule family decided that version's main result and let Essentia key a pitchless drum track (the service's own drum kit at 128 BPM, kit seed 3). None of them is used here.
- A second version read the plan's "librosa checked" as librosa shown only, where the first had read it as a veto; the change was made after the numbers had been seen. It let through a pitch-class level of 0.1, which gave keys to one pitched source (a one-note bass over drums, a church bell, a tabla), and a tolerance of 0.04 that librosa's clips reject. The veto is restored here, and no loosening is shipped.
- The evaluation clips were scored in each version: the first time from measurements rounded to six decimals, which made a 0.04 tolerance look as if it fixed a librosa control (one third was 0.96 of the tempo to the last bit). The values here follow from the written procedure on the full-precision recording, applying only changes that withhold more; none was chosen or changed in response to an evaluation score.
- Before the plan was written, earlier reports had shown why 39 of 50 GiantSteps+ keys were withheld, some of them on clips that are now evaluation clips; that is what drew attention to the key evidence rules.
- The pitchless controls drawn at 30 seeds each (two drum kits, noise with a beat, a siren over drums, clicks) were added after the drum track above was found, and the one-pitch-class ones (a one-note bass, octave riffs, bells alone and over drums, tabla, toms, a cowbell, a held synth note) after the level of 0.1 was. No value had been set on them; the choice sees them like the other controls.
- The single-source rule (no key for one pitched source: spectral lines that keep their proportions over the window, rise and fall together and spell one note, or two that are not a third or a sixth apart, as a cowbell or one note repeated on a bass) was added after this calibration, from the one-pitch-class and chord controls and the GiantSteps+ keys the service gave without it. The procedure does not vary it: before is without it, after with it at the values each run recorded, and the choice below is made without it. The held-out controls are evaluation only: the choice does not see them.
- Added to the controls after the single-source rule, with no value set on them: two rack toms and a floor tom over a kit, a tubular bell, triads in a soft timbre (an electric piano, a flute, an FM electric piano or a triangle wave) and triads struck on bars (a marimba-like bar or a glockenspiel). The rule was not changed for them.
- The families are taken in turn, each with the earlier choices in place: steadiness check: librosa's tempo on each third's onsets; steadiness check: tolerance; steadiness check: beat drift within a third (window, tolerance); tempo confidence minimum; onset periodicity minimum (tempo); key evidence: share of energy in spectral lines (no sustained pitch), of the mix or of its harmonic part; key evidence: pitch classes (fewer than three), by vector and level; key strength minimum.
- Before means the service's values at commit `ec7d24b`; after means the values the runs were made with (commit `2901b7f`, `9682a4a`).

### Clips

| data | rules | estimators | calibration | evaluation | command |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ballroom (ISMIR 2004 tempo induction contest) | tempo | `librosa`, `essentia`, `beat-this` | 99 | 101 | `uv run python -m evals_audio --suite calibration --dataset ballroom --limit 200` |
| GiantSteps Key, as GiantSteps+ (Faraldo 2017 revision) | key | `librosa`, `essentia` | 125 | 125 | `uv run python -m evals_audio --suite calibration --dataset giantsteps-key --limit 250 --seed 1` |
| generated controls | tempo, key, gate | `librosa`, `essentia`, `beat-this` | 2076 | – | `uv run python -m evals_audio --suite controls` |

Each calibration run records, per clip and estimator, what the service served and the estimate with every rule lifted, with the measurements each rule compares (the confidence, onset periodicity, each third's beat and onset tempo, the beat drift; the key strength, the share of energy in spectral lines, the pitch classes they spell and the pitch track's verdicts). Its scores against the reference are kept; the reference is not.

Derivation check: re-derived from the recorded measurements at the service's own values, the decision differs from what the service served on 0 of 11480 clip and estimator pairs. Records that failed and are left out of every table: 0.

### Held-out evaluation (evaluation clips)

The rules before against the rules after, on the same evaluation clips: correct / n (tempo Acc1, key exact; a withheld clip counts as not correct), the paired exact McNemar test on the clips only one side gets right, and the answers the change adds or withholds with how often they are right. Gross errors as above.

### Controls

Generated signals whose truth is known by construction: the issue's lists of defects found with generated probes, and the probe sets of the service's own tests (`services/audio/tests`). Groups: **no-key**: no key: atonal, inharmonic or pitchless; **one-source**: no key: one held pitch or one voice, or one pitched source over drums; **pitchless-seeded**: no key: drums, noise with a beat, a siren over drums and clicks, 30 seeds each, drawn from the seed (tempo, kit, levels, sounds); **one-pitch-class-seeded**: no key: one pitched source (a one-note bass, octave riffs, a bell alone or over drums, tabla, toms, a cowbell, a held synth note, two rack toms and a floor tom over a kit, a tubular bell), 30 seeds each; **music**: music the service's tests expect a key for; **no-tempo**: no single tempo; **tempo**: a known tempo (octave and meter); **key**: a known key the service lost or changed; **noise**: noise: nothing to measure; **chord-keyed-seeded**: a key: chords that keep the key the other rules give them (held chords faded, swelling, getting louder, with tremolo or a sidechain pump, C major stabs, a bass and chord riff on one chord, one-bar I-V-vi-IV riffs, 30 seeds each; triads with the third 1.5-6 dB softer than a doubled root at every root, mode and three timbres; seventh chords whose fifth folds into a doubled root and whose third is quiet; a third 10-25 cents off over a doubled root; sus2 and sus4 chords, their key not scored; triads in a soft timbre (an electric piano, a flute, an FM electric piano or a triangle wave) and triads struck on bars (a marimba-like bar or a glockenspiel), 30 seeds each); **held-out-seeded**: evaluation only: the one-pitch-class families at seeds 600-629 and the chord families at 800-829; no rule value is chosen on them. A truth of *none* means there is nothing to measure; *a key* means music in more than one key (any key is right, none is wrong). The values are derived before and after from the recorded measurements, as for the annotated clips.

### Octave and meter errors and the confidence

Among the served tempos that are right forgiving octave and meter (Acc2), whether the confidence ranks the ones within 4 % (Acc1) above the octave and meter errors: the AUC is the chance that an Acc1-right tempo has the higher confidence (0.5 is no ranking), with a one-sided permutation p-value. A minimum on the confidence can only remove octave errors where it ranks them.

| clips | data | estimator | Acc1 right / octave or meter error | AUC | test |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| calibration | ballroom | `librosa` | 53 / 13 | 0.66 | p = 0.037 (Monte Carlo, 100,000 permutations) |
| calibration | ballroom | `essentia` | 49 / 11 | 0.75 | p = 0.005 (Monte Carlo, 100,000 permutations) |
| evaluation | ballroom | `librosa` | 56 / 13 | 0.71 | p = 0.009 (Monte Carlo, 100,000 permutations) |
| evaluation | ballroom | `essentia` | 57 / 12 | 0.60 | p = 0.152 (Monte Carlo, 100,000 permutations) |

### The noise gate

| data | clips | gated as noise or silence |
|---|---|---|
| ballroom | 200 | 0 |
| giantsteps-key | 250 | 1 |

Noise controls gated: 5 of 11 (whistling-noise-bands, pink-noise-hum-whine, white-noise, pink-noise, brown-noise); passed: babble-2, babble-4, brown-noise-hum, siren-in-pink-noise, engine-rev, kettle. None of those that pass gets a tempo or a key. Nothing here measures what a stricter gate would cost on speech, a cappella or solo instruments (the annotated sets hold none), so the gate's values are not changed by this calibration.

## Tempo: Ballroom (ISMIR 2004 tempo induction contest)

```sh
cd evals/audio && uv run python -m evals_audio --suite tempo --dataset ballroom --limit 200
```

- Run 2026-10-29T01:57:02Z on commit `9682a4a`; 330 s wall with 4 worker processes.
- Analyzer: `ANALYZER_VERSION` 2.3.4 `2.3.4+librosa`, `2.3.4+essentia`, `2.3.4+beat-this` (the fingerprint models were not loaded; tempo and key do not use them).
- Environment: python 3.12.3, librosa 1.0.0, numpy 2.3.5, scipy 1.16.3, mirdata 1.0.0, essentia-tensorflow 2.1b6.dev1389, beat-this 1.1.0, torch 2.11.0+cpu.
- Seeds: selection 1, clip windows 1, bootstrap 1. Selection: 200 of 698 usable tracks drawn with numpy default_rng(1). Clips: 30 s, the start fixed by the clip seed and the track id (SHA-256); a recording of 30 s or less is used whole. The 200 recordings measured are 29.4-31.8 s long (used whole: 4).

### Dataset

|  |  |
|---|---|
| licence (annotations) | CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-sa/4.0/), per mirdata 1.0.0 (ballroom.LICENSE_INFO); not stated by the dataset: the contest page states no licence |
| audio | commercial excerpts (BallroomDancers.com, credited on the contest page); no licence is stated for the audio. Fetched into the local data directory for evaluation only and never redistributed: the repository holds no audio and no audio file names. |
| source: audio | https://mtg.upf.edu/ismir2004/contest/tempoContest/data1.tar.gz |
| source: tempo | https://mtg.upf.edu/ismir2004/contest/tempoContest/data2.tar.gz |
| source: index | https://zenodo.org/records/13993346 (mirdata index 1.0) |
| reference | the contest's ground-truth tempo (one value per excerpt) |
| tracks in the index | 698 |
| usable references | 698 |
| selected | 200 |
| audio verified (MD5) | 200 |
| audio unavailable | 0 |
| audio fetched from | 200 from https://mtg.upf.edu/ismir2004/contest/tempoContest/data1.tar.gz |
| data directory | 1.6 GB (cap 2.0 GB) |

- 698 excerpts of about 30 s from BallroomDancers.com, eight dance styles, so the 30 s window covers nearly all of each excerpt.
- Published Ballroom figures use all 698 excerpts; a sample of n clips is only comparable within its interval.
- Ballroom's track ids are its file names (album names included), so the results use the first 10 hex digits of a SHA-256 of the dataset name and the id; `uv run python -m evals_audio --lookup ID --dataset ballroom` maps one back from the local index.

Credits (cite these when using the data):

- Gouyon, F., Klapuri, A., Dixon, S., Alonso, M., Tzanetakis, G., Uhle, C., Cano, P. (2006). An experimental comparison of audio tempo induction algorithms. IEEE Transactions on Audio, Speech, and Language Processing 14(5), 1832-1844. doi:10.1109/TSA.2005.858509
- Foscarin, F., Schlüter, J., Widmer, G. (2024). Beat this! Accurate beat tracking without DBN postprocessing. Proc. ISMIR 2024. arXiv:2407.21658. https://github.com/CPJKU/beat_this: the code and the published model weights (checkpoint final0, from the authors' cloud space) are under the MIT licence

### Accuracy

- `librosa` returns a tempo for 139 of 200 clips (69.5 %); the tempo it returns is within 4 % of the reference for 109 of all 200 clips (54.5 %).
- `essentia` returns a tempo for 133 of 200 clips (66.5 %); the tempo it returns is within 4 % of the reference for 106 of all 200 clips (53.0 %).
- `beat-this` returns a tempo for 153 of 200 clips (76.5 %); the tempo it returns is within 4 % of the reference for 148 of all 200 clips (74.0 %).

- beat-this's checkpoint was trained on every dataset its authors used except GTZAN, Ballroom included (CPJKU/beat_this_annotations lists ballroom; the authors warn that results on training data are unfairly good). Its Ballroom numbers are on its own training data: they are shown, marked, but they say nothing about new music and are kept out of the bake-off conclusion (its pair tables on this dataset are not printed).

| estimator | n | answered (coverage) | withheld | failed | Acc1: correct / n | Acc1: correct / answered | Acc2: correct / n | Acc2: correct / answered |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `librosa` | 200 | 139/200 = 69.5 % [62.8, 75.5] | 61 | 0 | 109/200 = 54.5 % [47.6, 61.3] | 109/139 = 78.4 % [70.9, 84.4] | 135/200 = 67.5 % [60.7, 73.6] | 135/139 = 97.1 % [92.8, 98.9] |
| `essentia` | 200 | 133/200 = 66.5 % [59.7, 72.7] | 67 | 0 | 106/200 = 53.0 % [46.1, 59.8] | 106/133 = 79.7 % [72.1, 85.7] | 129/200 = 64.5 % [57.7, 70.8] | 129/133 = 97.0 % [92.5, 98.8] |
| `beat-this` (trained on this dataset) | 200 | 153/200 = 76.5 % [70.2, 81.8] | 47 | 0 | 148/200 = 74.0 % [67.5, 79.6] | 148/153 = 96.7 % [92.6, 98.6] | 148/200 = 74.0 % [67.5, 79.6] | 148/153 = 96.7 % [92.6, 98.6] |

### Paired comparisons

Each pair of estimators on the same clips: the clips both answered, and all n clips with a withheld answer counted as not correct. The columns above are not comparable between estimators that answer different clips; compare them here.

`librosa` and `essentia`:

| metric | clips | `librosa` | `essentia` | pairs better for `librosa` / for `essentia` | test |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Acc1 | both answered (n = 123) | 102 | 102 | 2 / 2 | McNemar exact p = 1.000 |
| Acc1 | all n (n = 200) | 109 | 106 | 9 / 6 | McNemar exact p = 0.607 |
| Acc2 | both answered (n = 123) | 120 | 120 | 0 / 0 | McNemar exact p = 1.000 |
| Acc2 | all n (n = 200) | 135 | 129 | 15 / 9 | McNemar exact p = 0.307 |

Pairs with `beat-this` are not shown: it was trained on this dataset, so a comparison here says nothing about new music.

### Withheld

`librosa`, withheld 61: onsets do not repeat enough (periodicity under the minimum): 26; confidence under the estimator's minimum: 23; tempo does not hold over the window: 12. The estimator still had an estimate for 61 of them.
`essentia`, withheld 67: confidence under the estimator's minimum: 29; onsets do not repeat enough (periodicity under the minimum): 26; tempo does not hold over the window: 12. The estimator still had an estimate for 67 of them.
`beat-this`, withheld 47: onsets do not repeat enough (periodicity under the minimum): 25; tempo does not hold over the window: 17; no estimate: 5. The estimator still had an estimate for 42 of them.

When onsets that do not repeat enough (the periodicity minimum), a confidence under the estimator's minimum or a tempo that does not hold over the window withholds an answer, the estimator usually still has an estimate: the harness recomputes it with the rule lifted and scores it against the reference, which shows what each rule costs. A rule that helps withholds estimates that are right less often than the ones it returns. These estimates never reach a user and are not counted in the tables above. The first row per estimator is what the service returned, as served. A rule's row counts the clips that this rule alone withholds, so its last column is what correct / n would be with that rule alone lifted; clips that several rules withhold have their own row, and the last row lifts every rule. Clips with no estimate even with every rule lifted (the gate, no estimate) stay at no correct value.

### Accuracy vs coverage

Answered clips ranked by the service's confidence, highest first; each row keeps the top k (coverage = k / n) and gives correct / k with its interval. Rows with fewer than 10 clips are left out, except the last row (every answered clip). Neighbouring rows share most of their clips and their intervals overlap, so a rise or fall between rows is not evidence on its own. Whether the confidence ranks answers at all is the AUC line under each table: the chance that a correct answer has a higher confidence than a wrong one (0.5 is random order), with a one-sided Mann-Whitney p-value against random order.

`librosa`:

## Key: GiantSteps Key, as GiantSteps+ (Faraldo 2017 revision)

```sh
cd evals/audio && uv run python -m evals_audio --suite key --dataset giantsteps-key --limit 250 --seed 1
```

- Run 2026-10-29T14:10:11Z on commit `2901b7f`; 116 s wall with 4 worker processes.
- Analyzer: `ANALYZER_VERSION` 2.3.4 `2.3.4+librosa`, `2.3.4+essentia` (the fingerprint models were not loaded; tempo and key do not use them).
- Environment: python 3.12.3, librosa 1.0.0, numpy 2.3.5, scipy 1.16.3, mirdata 1.0.0, essentia-tensorflow 2.1b6.dev1389.
- Seeds: selection 1, clip windows 1, bootstrap 1. Selection: 250 of 476 usable tracks drawn with numpy default_rng(1). Clips: 30 s, the start fixed by the clip seed and the track id (SHA-256); a recording of 30 s or less is used whole. The 250 recordings measured are 44.8-120.1 s long (used whole: 0).

### Dataset

|  |  |
|---|---|
| licence (annotations) | CC BY-SA 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/), stated in the Zenodo record (10.5281/zenodo.1095691); mirdata 1.0.0 agrees |
| audio | commercial excerpts (Beatport two-minute previews; the record says the audio belongs to Beatport); no licence is stated for the audio. Fetched into the local data directory for evaluation only and never redistributed: the repository holds no audio and no audio file names. |
| source: audio | https://zenodo.org/records/1095691/files/audio.zip |
| source: keys | https://zenodo.org/records/1095691/files/keys.zip |
| source: metadata | https://zenodo.org/records/1095691/files/original_metadata.zip |
| source: index | https://zenodo.org/records/13993357 (mirdata index '+') |
| reference | the GiantSteps+ single-key label (keys_gs+), tonic and major/minor |
| tracks in the index | 600 |
| usable references | 476 |
| not scored (whole dataset) | mode other than major/minor: 26, no mode: 3, two keys: 93, unparseable: 2 |
| selected | 250 |
| audio verified (MD5) | 250 |
| audio unavailable | 0 |
| audio fetched from | 250 from https://zenodo.org/records/1095691/files/audio.zip |
| data directory | 1.6 GB (cap 2.0 GB) |

- 600 two-minute Beatport previews of electronic dance music. mirdata's giantsteps_key loader is the GiantSteps+ revision of the original GiantSteps Key labels, with the audio published on Zenodo.
- Labels with two keys ('E major | E minor'), 'other' modes, 'X' or a misspelt tonic are not scored and are counted as unusable; modal labels keep their major/minor word ('F minor dorian' is scored as F minor).
- Published GiantSteps Key figures are computed on the whole two-minute previews, mostly against the original 2015 labels; here one 30 s window per preview is scored against GiantSteps+ with the ambiguous labels left out. Not comparable.

Credits (cite these when using the data):

- Faraldo, Á. (2017). GiantSteps+ EDM Key Dataset. Zenodo. doi:10.5281/zenodo.1095691
- Faraldo, Á. (2017). Tonality Estimation in Electronic Dance Music: A Computational and Musically Informed Examination. PhD thesis, Universitat Pompeu Fabra, Barcelona
- Knees, P., Faraldo, Á., Herrera, P., Vogl, R., Böck, S., Hörschläger, F., Le Goff, M. (2015). Two data sets for tempo estimation and key detection in electronic dance music annotated from user corrections. Proc. ISMIR 2015, 364-370. doi:10.5281/zenodo.1414996 (the original GiantSteps Key dataset)
- The record's description asks for 10.5281/zenodo.1101082, but that DOI resolves to a different record (the Beatport EDM Key Dataset, 1,486 excerpts); the record used here is cited above

### Accuracy

- `librosa` returns a key for 48 of 250 clips (19.2 %); the key it returns is the reference key for 32 of all 250 clips (12.8 %).
- `essentia` returns a key for 48 of 250 clips (19.2 %); the key it returns is the reference key for 36 of all 250 clips (14.4 %).

When the evidence rule or a strength under the minimum withholds an answer, the estimator usually still has an estimate: the harness recomputes it with the rule lifted and scores it against the reference, which shows what each rule costs. A rule that helps withholds estimates that are right less often than the ones it returns. These estimates never reach a user and are not counted in the tables above. The first row per estimator is what the service returned, as served. A rule's row counts the clips that this rule alone withholds, so its last column is what correct / n would be with that rule alone lifted; clips that several rules withhold have their own row, and the last row lifts every rule. Clips with no estimate even with every rule lifted (the gate, no estimate) stay at no correct value.

### Accuracy vs coverage

Answered clips ranked by the service's confidence, highest first; each row keeps the top k (coverage = k / n) and gives correct / k with its interval. Rows with fewer than 10 clips are left out, except the last row (every answered clip). Neighbouring rows share most of their clips and their intervals overlap, so a rise or fall between rows is not evidence on its own. Whether the confidence ranks answers at all is the AUC line under each table: the chance that a correct answer has a higher confidence than a wrong one (0.5 is random order), with a one-sided Mann-Whitney p-value against random order.

`librosa`:

| coverage | k | min. confidence | MIREX | exact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10.0 % | 25 | 0.717 | 21.1/25 = 84.4 % [69.7, 93.6] | 19/25 = 76.0 % [56.6, 88.5] |
| 19.2 % | 48 | 0.414 | 35.8/48 = 74.6 % [62.9, 84.1] | 32/48 = 66.7 % [52.5, 78.3] |

Confidence ranking: exact AUC 0.69 (32 correct, 16 wrong; p = 0.017, Monte Carlo, 100,000 permutations).

`essentia`:

| coverage | k | min. confidence | MIREX | exact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10.0 % | 25 | 0.861 | 22.1/25 = 88.4 % [74.0, 97.2] | 21/25 = 84.0 % [65.3, 93.6] |
| 19.2 % | 48 | 0.619 | 38.8/48 = 80.8 % [69.6, 89.2] | 36/48 = 75.0 % [61.2, 85.1] |

Confidence ranking: exact AUC 0.70 (36 correct, 12 wrong; p = 0.017, Monte Carlo, 100,000 permutations).

## Reproducing

Datasets are fetched into `~/liner-notes-data` (or `LINER_NOTES_DATA`), outside the repository, under a cap (`LINER_NOTES_DATA_CAP_BYTES`, 2 GB by default). Only the selected tracks' audio is written, each checked against mirdata's MD5. See `evals/audio/README.md`.
